# Odyssey driver routines of `libGLcore.so`, read one by one

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, N32 `libGLcore.so` for IP30 (`usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`; the IP35 copy and the patch builds carry the same routines where their code hashes agree in `docs/inventory/functions.tsv`). Each entry names a routine of the board-specific `ODYSSEY` directory (`libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/`), its address and size, and says what it computes. Labels are **established** for routines whose whole body was read in the Ghidra decompilation (`$gp` and jump tables resolved with `ghidra/scripts/IrixGp.java` and `IrixJumpTables.java`) next to the assembly, and **inferred** for statements that rest on a callee that was not read. Offsets are into the Odyssey GL context record (the record the library keeps its current context in, address 0x1010 of the process), unless an entry says another record. The routines that move pixels, draw and copy pixel rectangles, transfer depth and stencil, and set the raster position belong to other chapters and are not read here. The same routines in the other builds are listed in [libglcore-odyssey-builds.md](libglcore-odyssey-builds.md).

Words sent to the board are the class-1 command words of `BUZZ.h`: `0x00010000 | COUNT << 10 | REG`, followed by COUNT operands. "The settle sequence" is the group `0x10443` with operand 250, `0x10046` twice and `0x10019`, `0x10443` with operand 150 and `0x10046` four times, and `0x10443` with operand 250 and `0x10046` twice, as in `docs/opengl.md` (the X module's draw routines send the same words). Writes go to the command-FIFO window the library maps at process address 0x10000.

Dispatch-table slots. The context holds a pointer at +0x17f4 to a table of 4-byte entry points in the order of the GL entry names (`__glim_Vertex2f` calls slot +0x534, the `Vertex2fv` entry, with a pointer to two floats). Names given for slots below are **inferred** from that order together with the width and count of the arguments each caller passes.

## N32 command senders read from the 6.5.30 media build

Constant names used by the sender entries below, **inferred** from the public GL headers; the code shows only the values: 0x404 `GL_FRONT`, 0x405 `GL_BACK`, 0x1201 `GL_DIFFUSE`, 0x1202 `GL_SPECULAR`, 0x1601 `GL_SHININESS`, 0x1603 `GL_COLOR_INDEXES`, 0x81fa `GL_SEPARATE_SPECULAR_COLOR`, 0x1c00 `GL_RENDER`, 0x1c02 `GL_SELECT` and 0x1d00 `GL_FLAT`. In the sender names, `Bm` is read as two faces and `Fm` as the front face only, `T` as texture, `N` as normal and `Fast` as the form that keeps flag bytes in the context; these readings are **inferred** from the code each name labels.

- `CISendFc` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e9f40, 100 bytes): **established.**
  Writes one colour-index vertex packet to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 from the record in argument 0: the format word 0x2f00c02e, or 0x3000c02e when the byte at argument 0 +0xb8 is zero (`movz`; `sw` at 0xd9e9f60), token 0xc580c407 (`sw` at 0xd9e9f68) with the float at +0x90, and position token 0x8080d005 (`sw` at 0xd9e9f7c) with the floats at +0x10, +0x14, +0x18 and +0x1c. The routine has no branch, calls nothing and defines no result.
- `RGBSendUnclippedBmFmTNFast` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e9270, 1124 bytes): **established.**
  This fast two-face textured unclipped sender sends up to two material groups and then one vertex packet to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 from the record in argument 0. When the byte at argument 0 +0x190 is zero it writes the back group 0xca80d035 at 0xd9e9470, 0xcc80d037 at 0xd9e949c, 0xcb80d036 at 0xd9e94c8 and 0xcd80d038 at 0xd9e94f4 from the record at argument 0 +0x120, followed by a `__glOdyim_Materialfv` call (`jalr` at 0xd9e952c) with 0x405, 0x1601 and a pointer to the float at record +0x40, and sets the context byte at +0x168c5 to 1, and then the front group 0xc680d02f at 0xd9e9398, 0xc880d031 at 0xd9e93c4, 0xc780d030 at 0xd9e93f0 and 0xc980d032 at 0xd9e941c from the record at argument 0 +0xd0, followed by a `__glOdyim_Materialfv` call (`jalr` at 0xd9e9454) with 0x404, 0x1601 and a pointer to the float at record +0x40, and sets the context byte at +0x168c4 to 1. When that byte is nonzero it skips a face whose context byte is zero; for a face whose byte is nonzero it writes the back group 0xca80d035 at 0xd9e92bc, 0xcc80d037 at 0xd9e92e8, 0xcb80d036 at 0xd9e9314 and 0xcd80d038 at 0xd9e9340 from the record at context +0x1686c, followed by a `__glOdyim_Materialfv` call (`jalr` at 0xd9e9378) with 0x405, 0x1601 and a pointer to the float at record +0x40, and clears the context byte at +0x168c5, and then the front group 0xc680d02f at 0xd9e9558, 0xc880d031 at 0xd9e9584, 0xc780d030 at 0xd9e95b0 and 0xc980d032 at 0xd9e95dc from the record at context +0x1681c, followed by a `__glOdyim_Materialfv` call (`jalr` at 0xd9e9614) with 0x404, 0x1601 and a pointer to the float at record +0x40, and clears the context byte at +0x168c4. The vertex packet is the format word 0x2f00c02e, or 0x3000c02e when the byte at argument 0 +0xb8 is zero (`movz`; `sw` at 0xd9e963c), normal token 0xc180cc19 (`sw` at 0xd9e9644) with the floats at argument 0 +0xc0, +0xc4 and +0xc8, texture token 0xc380d025 (`sw` at 0xd9e9668) with +0x30, +0x34, +0x38 and +0x3c, and position token 0x8080d005 (`sw` at 0xd9e9694) with +0x10, +0x14, +0x18 and +0x1c.
- `RGBSendClippedBmFmTNFast` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e9ca0, 672 bytes): **established.**
  This fast two-face textured clipped sender writes one vertex packet from the record in argument 0 to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000, starting with the format word 0x2f00c02e, or 0x3000c02e when the byte at argument 0 +0xb8 is zero (`movz`; `sw` at 0xd9e9cec). It loads the context word at +0x588 and compares it with 0x81fa (`lw` at 0xd9e9cf8, `beq` at 0xd9e9d0c), and the public GL value 0x81fa is `GL_SEPARATE_SPECULAR_COLOR`, so the word is **inferred** to be a colour-control mode. When the word differs the 8 material tokens that follow are: 0xc680d02f at 0xd9e9d14 takes four zeros, 0xc880d031 at 0xd9e9d28 takes four zeros, 0xc780d030 at 0xd9e9d3c takes zero, zero, zero and +0x9c, 0xc980d032 at 0xd9e9d54 takes +0x90, +0x94, +0x98 and zero, 0xca80d035 at 0xd9e9d74 takes four zeros, 0xcc80d037 at 0xd9e9d88 takes four zeros, 0xcb80d036 at 0xd9e9d9c takes zero, zero, zero and +0xac and 0xcd80d038 at 0xd9e9db4 takes +0xa0, +0xa4, +0xa8 and zero. - `__glOdysseyRasterPosGeneral` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ca3b0, 1452 bytes): **established.**
  Arguments: argument 0 is a record, argument 1 points to four floats and argument 2 is a small integer. The routine copies the four floats to argument 0 +0x100, +0x104, +0x108 and +0x10c (`swc1` at 0xd9ca3c4, 0xd9ca3d0, 0xd9ca3dc and 0xd9ca3f0) and stores 0 at argument 0 +0xf0 (`sw` at 0xd9ca3e8). Argument 2 selects the work (`beq` at 0xd9ca3ec for 2, 0xd9ca3f8 for 3 and 0xd9ca404 for 4); any other value returns at once (`jr ra` at 0xd9ca410). Each of the three cases calls a function pointer stored at +0xf8 (argument 2 = 2; `jalr` at 0xd9ca478), +0xfc (3; 0xd9ca510) or +0x100 (4; 0xd9ca424) of the record whose pointer is at argument 0 +0x7414, passing argument 0 +0x160, argument 0 +0x100 and that record +0xb0 as the first three arguments. The case then calls `__glClipCheckAll` (`jalr` at 0xd9ca498, 0xd9ca530 and 0xd9ca444) with argument 0, argument 0 +0xf0 and a data address (0xdae1050 for 2, 0xdae120c for 3, 0xdae13d0 for 4), which it also stores at argument 0 +0xfc (`sw` at 0xd9ca49c, 0xd9ca534 and 0xd9ca448). When `__glClipCheckAll` gives a nonzero result the routine clears the byte at argument 0 +0x1c4 and returns (`sb` at 0xd9ca550, 0xd9ca4b8 and 0xd9ca464, in the return delay slots). When it gives zero it stores 1 into that byte (`sb` at 0xd9ca4c4) and continues. It then loads the word at process address 0x1018 (`lw` at 0xd9ca4dc) and the context word at +0x16964 (`lw` at 0xd9ca4d4). A value at 0x1018 other than 0 or 2 makes it tail-call `__glSetError` with 0x502 (`jr` at 0xd9ca4fc). When the word at 0x1018 is 2, or is 0 and the context word is nonzero, it stores 1 into the context byte at +0x16975 (`sb` at 0xd9ca558), calls the function pointer at context +0x2e40 with the context as its argument (`jalr` at 0xd9ca560) and stores 0 at 0x1018 (`sw` at 0xd9ca570). Unless it tail-called `__glSetError`, it then writes four records to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000, each a token followed by sixteen zero words: 0x3cf8 (`sw` at 0xd9ca578), 0x3cf9 (0xd9ca5c0), 0x3cfa (0xd9ca608) and 0x3cfb (0xd9ca650). It sets the byte at argument 0 +0xa73c to 1 (`sb` at 0xd9ca69c) and reads the word at argument 0 +0xc84 (`lw` at 0xd9ca6a0). **Inferred** from the public GL headers: 0x1c00 is `GL_RENDER` and 0x1c02 is `GL_SELECT`, so this is the render-mode word. When that word is not 0x1c00 (`beq` at 0xd9ca6a8) it copies the floats at argument 0 +0xd0..+0xdc to +0x110..+0x11c (`swc1` at 0xd9ca6c4 through 0xd9ca6d8); when the byte at +0xc94 is nonzero (`beq` at 0xd9ca6d4) it also copies +0xc0..+0xcc to +0x180..+0x18c and +0xe0..+0xec to +0x120..+0x12c (`swc1` at 0xd9ca6f0 through 0xd9ca720). The byte-zero case takes other copies at 0xd9ca94c, not read here. When the word is 0x1c00 control goes to 0xd9ca798. If any bit of 0x3c0040 is set in the word at argument 0 +0x700 (`bne` at 0xd9ca7ac), or if any of sixteen floats at the pointer taken from argument 0 +0x7428 differs from its constant (a `c.eq.d` chain from 0xd9ca7c4 to 0xd9ca930), it calls `__glOdysseyUpdateRasterPos` with the render-mode word as its third argument (`jalr` at 0xd9ca93c) and rejoins the common code below. Otherwise it branches to 0xd9ca76c. The common code at 0xd9ca724 calls the pointer at argument 0 +0xfc with argument 0, argument 0 +0xf0 and the word at +0x6de4 ORed with 0x1d (`jalr` at 0xd9ca748), after copying the word at +0x2f24 over the word at +0x2f1c (`sw` at 0xd9ca744); it restores +0x2f1c afterwards (`sw` at 0xd9ca75c) and clears the byte at +0xa73c (`sb` at 0xd9ca760). At 0xd9ca76c, when the render-mode word is 0x1c02, it calls `__glSelectPoint` (`jalr` at 0xd9ca778); otherwise it returns. Which board registers the 0x3cf8 to 0x3cfb records address, and what the context words at +0x16964 and +0x16975 hold, are **unknown**.
- `RGBSendUnclippedBmFmTN` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e7e40, 672 bytes): **established.**

- `__glOdysseyPushLoop` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xdacc570, 308 bytes): **established.**

The count is split by its bits (`srl` at 0xdacc580, `andi` at 0xdacc588 and the later `andi`/`srl` pairs): bits 5 and up give the number of passes of the main loop, bit 4 selects one 64-byte block, bits 1 to 3 give a number of 64-bit words and bit 0 an odd final 32-bit word. Each main-loop pass copies 128 bytes, as two blocks of eight 64-bit words: sixteen `ld` (at 0xdacc590 through 0xdacc5ac and 0xdacc5d4 through 0xdacc5f0) and sixteen `sd` to the FIFO window (at 0xdacc5b0 through 0xdacc5cc and 0xdacc5f4 through 0xdacc610), then the source advances by 0x80 (`addiu` at 0xdacc618) and the loop repeats while the pass count, decremented at 0xdacc5d0, is nonzero (`bne` at 0xdacc614). The routine issues `pref` hints 128, 256 and 384 bytes ahead of the source (at 0xdacc574, 0xdacc578 and 0xdacc58c). When bit 4 of the count is set it copies one 64-byte block of eight 64-bit words (`ld` at 0xdacc628 through 0xdacc644, `sd` at 0xdacc648 through 0xdacc664) and advances the source by 0x40. It then copies one 64-bit word per iteration for the count in bits 1 to 3 (`ld` at 0xdacc678, `sd` at 0xdacc680, `bne` at 0xdacc684), and when bit 0 is set one last 32-bit word (`lw` at 0xdacc694, `sw` at 0xdacc698). ## Vertex-array and indexed-colour routines read from the body

In the vertex-array routine names, `C3F`, `N3F`, `N3S`, `T2F` and `V3F` are read as three colour floats, three normal floats, three normal shorts, two texture-coordinate floats and three vertex floats, `Int` as one interleaved array and `Sep` as separate arrays; this is **inferred** from the names together with the load widths and strides in the code.

- `CISendUnclippedBmFmN` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ea130, 576 bytes): **established.**
  This two-face colour-index sender passes front and back material parameters to `__glOdyim_Materialfv` and then writes one vertex packet to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 from the record in argument 0; two `movz` (at 0xd9ea15c and 0xd9ea1a8) on the byte at argument 0 +0x190 pick the front record, argument 0 +0xd0 when the byte is zero and context +0x1681c otherwise, and the back record, argument 0 +0x120 or context +0x1686c. For the front record the calls pass 0x404 as the first argument and 0x1201 (`jalr` at 0xd9ea1b0), 0x1202 (0xd9ea1e4), 0x1601 (0xd9ea200) and 0x1603 (0xd9ea22c) as the second; the pointed-to array holds the floats at record +0x10 through +0x1c, +0x20 through +0x2c, the float at +0x40, and the floats at +0x44, +0x4c and +0x48 respectively. For the back record the calls pass 0x405 as the first argument and 0x1201 (`jalr` at 0xd9ea264), 0x1202 (0xd9ea298), 0x1601 (0xd9ea2b4) and 0x1603 (0xd9ea2e0) as the second; the pointed-to array holds the floats at record +0x10 through +0x1c, +0x20 through +0x2c, the float at +0x40, and the floats at +0x44, +0x4c and +0x48 respectively. The packet is the format word 0x2f00c02e, or 0x3000c02e when the byte at argument 0 +0xb8 is zero (`movz`; `sw` at 0xd9ea308), normal token 0xc180cc19 (`sw` at 0xd9ea310) with the floats at argument 0 +0xc0, +0xc4 and +0xc8, and position token 0x8080d005 (`sw` at 0xd9ea334) with +0x10, +0x14, +0x18 and +0x1c. It returns with the format word, 0x2f00c02e or 0x3000c02e, still in `v0`; that is a leftover, not a result.

- `__glOdyim_End` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c85f0, 204 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the word at process address 0x1018 (`lw` at 0xd9c85f8). When it is not 1, the routine calls `__glSetError` with 0x502 (`jalr` at 0xd9c8624) and returns. When it is 1 it writes 0x14001 to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 (`sw` at 0xd9c863c). If the context word at +0x96f4 is nonzero (`lw` at 0xd9c8644) it stores 0 into the context word at +0x9830 and writes 0x10443, 250, 0x10046 and 0x10046 (`sw` at 0xd9c8658, 0xd9c865c, 0xd9c8660 and 0xd9c8664). In either case it then stores 1 into +0x9830 (`sw` at 0xd9c866c). It then reads the context byte at +0x19dce (`lbu` at 0xd9c8674). When that byte is nonzero it stores 0 into +0x9830 again, writes the same four words 0x10443, 250, 0x10046 and 0x10046 (`sw` at 0xd9c868c, 0xd9c8690, 0xd9c8698 and 0xd9c869c) and clears the byte (`sb` at 0xd9c86a0). It ends by storing 0 at process address 0x1018 (`sw` at 0xd9c86a4) and returns. The words are the ones the settle sequence defined above starts with; the roles of the three context fields are **unknown**.

Otherwise it calls `__glOdysseyOpenPMObjWriteReference` (`jalr` at 0xd99166c) with the context, the words at argument 0 +0x10 and +0x14, a stack address (sp+16) and 1. A zero result makes it call `__glSetError` with 0x505 (`jalr` at 0xd99189c) and return. On success it stores 0xfff into the context words at +0x7c20 and +0x7c24 (`sw` at 0xd991680 and 0xd991684). It ORs 0x2000 into the context word at +0x16964 (`sw` at 0xd9916d8), 0x80 into +0x16960 (`sw` at 0xd991744) and later 0x2 into +0x16960 (`sw` at 0xd99176c), and stores 2 at process address 0x1018 after each of those updates (`sw` at 0xd9916e0, 0xd991748 and 0xd991770). Otherwise it stores the words it saved on entry back into context +0x7bfc, +0x7c5c and +0x969c through +0x96a8 (`sw` at 0xd991828 through 0xd991840), ORs 0x200082 into +0x16960 (`sw` at 0xd991858), stores 2 at 0x1018 (`sw` at 0xd991860) and returns. It saves registers in a 944-byte frame whose first 64-byte slots hold clipped vertex lists. It loads the word at argument 0 +0x570 and sets a flag when it equals 0x1d00 (`lw` at 0xd9ed938, `xori` at 0xd9ed950, `sltiu` at 0xd9ed95c); **Inferred** from the public GL headers: 0x1d00 is `GL_FLAT`. A result below 3 (`slti` at 0xd9ed980, `bne` at 0xd9ed98c) ends the routine at 0xd9edb14 without writing anything. Otherwise the result becomes the new count, the stack slot becomes the new vertex list and the slot pointer advances by 64 (`addiu` at 0xd9ed994). It then writes 0x14400 and 9 to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 (`sw` at 0xd9ed9c8 and 0xd9ed9d4) and, when the count is positive (`bgtz` at 0xd9ed9d0), processes each remaining vertex. When the flat flag is set it first calls the +0x19e9c pointer with the vertex and argument 0 (0xd9edb50). The byte at +0x191 of the vertex record then decides (`lbu` at 0xd9eda2c): when it is nonzero and the flag is clear, the routine stores the word at argument 0 +0xa6a8 into a table word at argument 0 +0xa698 plus 4 times a slot counter that cycles through 1, 2 and 3 (`sw` at 0xd9ed9e8; counter updated at 0xd9eda04 through 0xd9eda14) and calls the +0x19e94 pointer. When the loop is done it stores the +0xa6a8 word into the four table words at +0xa698 through +0xa6a4 (`sw` at 0xd9edaac, 0xd9edab0, 0xd9edab4 and 0xd9edabc). It finishes by writing 0x14001 (`sw` at 0xd9edac4). In either case it stores 1 into +0x9830 (`sw` at 0xd9edb04) and returns (`jr ra` at 0xd9edb0c). The per-vertex slot counter, the table at +0xa698 and the roles of the three pointers are **unknown**.

### Immediate vertex senders with a test on the transformed w

Small-w sender frame. Every `_smallw` routine below loads two process-global record pointers, from process address 0x1020 (A) and 0x1024 (B). It computes w' = x*A[+0x30] + y*A[+0x34] + z*A[+0x38] + w*A[+0x3c] on single-precision values (one `mul.s` and three `madd.s`), multiplies w' by the old float at B+8, and stores w' to B+8. The vertex goes to the fallback unless two tests pass: the product w'*(old B+8) must not be negative (`c.ueq.s` of its absolute value with itself, `bc1f` to the fallback), and |w'| must not be less than, or unordered with, the float at B+0 (`c.ult.s`, `bc1t` to the fallback). When both pass, the routine writes the position token and the four coordinates to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 and returns with `v0` holding 0x10000. The fallback loads the pointer at B+0x20 and jumps to it (`jr`), a tail call that leaves the arguments as the conversion left them. What A and B are, and what B+8 and B+0 hold, is **unknown**; the entries below record each variant's input conversion, token and addresses.

- `__glOdyim_Vertex4d_smallw` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xdacf880, 144 bytes): **established.**
  This double-precision scalar small-w sender takes four doubles in f12..f15 converted to single precision (`cvt.s.d` at 0xdacf880, 0xdacf884, 0xdacf888 and 0xdacf88c), forms w' from them, and writes token 0x8080d005 and the four converted floats when w' passes the two tests, otherwise tail-calling the fallback. The routine loads A and B at 0xdacf890 and 0xdacf894, computes w' with `mul.s` at 0xdacf8ac and `madd.s` at 0xdacf8b0, 0xdacf8b4 and 0xdacf8b8, stores it with `swc1` at 0xdacf8c4, runs the negative-product test (`c.ueq.s` at 0xdacf8cc, `bc1f` at 0xdacf8d4) and the limit test (`lwc1` at 0xdacf8a8, `c.ult.s` at 0xdacf8d8, `bc1t` at 0xdacf8e0), then writes the token (`sw` at 0xdacf8ec) and the values (`swc1` at 0xdacf8f0, 0xdacf8f4, 0xdacf8f8 and 0xdacf900), returns at 0xdacf8fc, and falls back through `lw` at 0xdacf904 and `jr` at 0xdacf908.

- `__glOdyim_Vertex4d_mag` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xdad0cc0, 228 bytes): **established.**
  Immediate-mode four-component vertex sender for four doubles in f12..f15, converted to single precision (`cvt.s.d` at 0xdad0cc0, 0xdad0cc4, 0xdad0cc8 and 0xdad0ccc), with a test of the transformed point against two planes. It loads two process-global record pointers, from process address 0x1020 (A; `lw` at 0xdad0cd0) and 0x1024 (B; `lw` at 0xdad0cd4), and computes w' = x*A[+0x30] + y*A[+0x34] + z*A[+0x38] + w*A[+0x3c] (`mul.s` at 0xdad0cec, `madd.s` at 0xdad0cf0, 0xdad0cf4 and 0xdad0cf8). It multiplies w' by the old float at B+8 and stores w' to B+8 (`swc1` at 0xdad0d04); it jumps to the fallback when that product is negative (`bc1f` at 0xdad0d1c). It then forms two plane values with the same four-term pattern, one from A+0x00..+0x0c (`madd.s` at 0xdad0d30, 0xdad0d34 and 0xdad0d38) and one from A+0x10..+0x1c (`madd.s` at 0xdad0d5c, 0xdad0d60 and 0xdad0d64), and compares each absolute value with |B[+4]*w'| (`c.ult.s`; `bc1t` at 0xdad0d48 and 0xdad0d74): a plane value larger than that bound, or unordered, goes to the fallback. When every test passes it writes token 0x8080d005 (`sw` at 0xdad0d80) and the four converted floats (`swc1` at 0xdad0d84, 0xdad0d88, 0xdad0d8c and 0xdad0d94) to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 and returns (`jr ra` at 0xdad0d90; `v0` holds 0x10000). The fallback loads the pointer at B+0x20 (`lw` at 0xdad0d98) and jumps to it (`jr` at 0xdad0d9c) as a tail call. The roles of A and B and of the plane fields are **unknown**.
- `__glOdyim_Vertex4d_mag_line0` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xdad2a80, 336 bytes): **established.**
  Immediate-mode four-component vertex sender for four doubles in f12..f15, converted to single precision (`cvt.s.d` at 0xdad2a80, 0xdad2a84, 0xdad2a88 and 0xdad2a8c), with a test of the transformed point against two planes. It loads two process-global record pointers, from process address 0x1020 (A; `lw` at 0xdad2a90) and 0x1024 (B; `lw` at 0xdad2a94), and computes w' = x*A[+0x30] + y*A[+0x34] + z*A[+0x38] + w*A[+0x3c] (`mul.s` at 0xdad2aac, `madd.s` at 0xdad2ab0, 0xdad2ab4 and 0xdad2ab8). It multiplies w' by the old float at B+8 and stores w' to B+8 (`swc1` at 0xdad2ac8); it jumps to the fallback when that product is negative (`bc1f` at 0xdad2ae0). It then forms two plane values with the same four-term pattern, one from A+0x00..+0x0c (`madd.s` at 0xdad2af4, 0xdad2af8 and 0xdad2afc) and one from A+0x10..+0x1c (`madd.s` at 0xdad2b54, 0xdad2b58 and 0xdad2b5c), and compares each absolute value with |B[+4]*w'| (`c.ult.s`; `bc1t` at 0xdad2b10 and 0xdad2b70): a plane value larger than that bound, or unordered, goes to the fallback. This variant adds a second check per plane: it adds B[+0xc] (first plane) or B[+0x10] (second plane) times w' to the plane value (`madd.s` at 0xdad2b18 and 0xdad2b78). A negative sum skips the next compare (`bc1f` at 0xdad2b28 and 0xdad2b88); otherwise the sum must not be less than, or unordered with, B[+0x18]*w' (`c.ult.s`; `bc1t` at 0xdad2b38 and 0xdad2b98), else the fallback runs. When every test passes it writes token 0x8080d005 (`sw` at 0xdad2bac) and the four converted floats (`swc1` at 0xdad2bb0, 0xdad2bb4, 0xdad2bb8 and 0xdad2bc0) to the command-FIFO window at process address 0x10000 and returns (`jr ra` at 0xdad2bbc; `v0` holds 0x10000). The fallback loads the pointer at B+0x20 (`lw` at 0xdad2bc4) and jumps to it (`jr` at 0xdad2bc8) as a tail call. The roles of A and B and of the plane fields are **unknown**.

- `__glOdysseyInitAsyncState` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd956030, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns immediately through its link register and does not read or write the context or board. The complete body is the two-instruction listing in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_async.s` at 0xd956030.
- `__glOdysseySPBalloc` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd976410, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the requested element count at argument record +0, clamps it to 1024 when smaller, writes that clamp back, multiplies the count by four, and calls the allocator function at argument object +0. It returns that allocator result; the full body is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_dlist.s` at 0xd976410.
- `__glOdysseySPBfree` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd976460, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Loads the free callback from argument object +12, calls it with the object as its only argument, preserves the global pointer across the call, and returns the callback result. The complete body is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_dlist.s` at 0xd976460.
- `__glOdysseySPCompMachdep` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9764a0, 116 bytes): **established.**
  Adds a sentinel to the record passed in argument +1, tests that record's word +20, and stores one of two internal callback addresses at record +8: the first when +20 is nonzero and the second otherwise. It returns no value. The complete branch and stores are in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_dlist.s` at 0xd9764a0.
- `__glOdysseyImageCalcSuitableHostFormatForDownload` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ac1f0, 144 bytes): **established.**
  Selects host format pairs from selector argument +2 and writes the format and type to the output pointers in arguments +3 and +4. Selectors 0 and 3 write format 6406 with types 5121 and 5123; selectors 16 and 17 write format 6400 with those same types; selector 16 uses 5121. Other selectors leave the outputs untouched. Every path returns 1. The full switch is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_image_convert.s` at 0xd9ac1f0.
- `__glOdysseyImageTilingFree` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c1720, 116 bytes): **established.**
  If the tiling record is non-null, it calls the allocator callback at +12 first with the record's pointer at +8, then clears record words +0, +4 and +8 and calls the same callback again to free the record itself. A null record returns without a call. The complete body is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_image_tiling.s` at 0xd9c1720.
- `__glOdysseyImageLogBase2` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c1e40, 64 bytes): **established.**
  Returns zero for input zero; otherwise counts right shifts until the input becomes zero and adds one when the original value was not a power of two. The full integer loop is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_image_utils.s` at 0xd9c1e40.
- `__glOdysseyPickDepthProcs` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c59e0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns immediately and changes no context state. The complete body is the two-instruction listing in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_pick.s` at 0xd9c59e0.
- `__glOdysseyPickPixelProcs` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c59f0, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Installs the board draw-pixels, convolution, separable-filter and slow separable-filter callbacks into context slots +12616, +12640, +12644 and +12648/+12652, then calls `__glOdysseySetCurrentFilter` with selector 2. The complete stores and call are in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_pick.s` at 0xd9c59f0.
- `__glOdysseyPickRenderBitmapProcs` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c5a50, 84 bytes): **established.**
  Always installs `__glOdysseyRenderBitmap` at context slot +12568. It selects the fast bitmap callback only when the byte at +1189 is nonzero and the three words at +1192, +1196 and +1200 are all zero; otherwise it installs the generic bitmap callback at +12564. The full conditional is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_pick.s` at 0xd9c5a50.
- `__glOdysseyPickTextureProcs` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c5ab0, 196 bytes): **established.**
  Chooses the texture-coordinate callback at context slot +12052 from the texture flag word at +1792. When the required flag bits and the two words at +1984 and +2108 permit hardware calculation, modes 9216, 9217 and 9218 select eye-linear, object-linear and sphere-map callbacks; the remaining accepted case selects the ordinary texture callback. All other branches select the mixed-texture callback. The complete tests are in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_pick.s` at 0xd9c5ab0.
- `__glOdysseyInitLUTs` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c3ae0, 360 bytes): **established.**
  Clears 20604 bytes at the process-global LUT area, then builds eight 2060-byte records with kind 6, element width 8, 16 entries, one component and a 256-element allocation request. It calls `__glOdysseyCreatePMObj` for each record and stops on the first failure; on success it sets the global completion byte. The full loop and failure path are in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_lut.s` at 0xd9c3ae0.
- `__glOdysseyImageChainFree` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd98f070, 148 bytes): **established.**
  Recursively frees the linked operation records whose next pointer is at +76, clears chain words +0, +4 and +8, and calls the allocator callback at the process-global object +12 with the chain record. A null chain skips all work. The complete recursion is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_image_chain.s` at 0xd98f070.
- `__glOdysseyImageCalcTexOffset` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd9bef40, 296 bytes): **established.**
  Reads descriptor words +44 and +48 and writes an offset code through argument +1. For the first word zero, second-word values 0, 3 and 4 produce code 0; for first word one, second word one produces code 0; for first word two, second-word values 0, 1 and 6 produce code 0 while value 2 produces code 2. Other combinations call `__glSetError` with 1282 and return 0. Successful combinations return 1. The complete decision tree is in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_image_move.s` at 0xd9bef40.
- `__glOdysseyInitFragmentMaterial` (`libGLcore.so`, 0xd985210, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns immediately without touching the context, board or its arguments. The complete body is the two-instruction listing in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_fragment.s` at 0xd985210.

## Validation and defaults

- `__glOdysseyValidateHardware` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd968180, 4992 bytes): **established.**
  Pushes every piece of changed context state to the board as class-1 blocks, choosing the blocks by the dirty word at +0x16960 (read at entry; set to 0 at exit). Before each block it tests the "settle needed" word at +0x9830 and, when set, clears it and sends the settle prefix `0x10443` 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`. The bit-to-block map, each block's operands taken from the context at the offset shown: bit 1 sends `0x10404` with the word at +0x7bfc and bit 2 sends `0x10405` with the word at +0x7c00; bit 0 sends `0x10c21` with three words from +0x7c34; bit 3 sends `0x1181a` with six words from +0x7c08; bit 4 sends `0x1081d` with the two words at +0x7c20; bit 5 sends `0x1041f` with the word at +0x7c2c; bit 6 sends `0x1084a` with the two words at +0x7c54; bit 7 sends `0x10452` with the word at +0x7c5c; bit 8 sends `0x1141b` with five words from +0x7cb0; bit 9 sends `0x11c16` with seven words from +0x7cc4; bit 13 sends `0x11847` with six words from +0x970c; bit 14 sends `0x11013` with four words from +0x9724; bit 15 sends `0x10c3e` with three words from +0x8d6c.
  The wider groups: bit 10 sends 64 level descriptors, blocks 0x280 to 0x2bf of three words each from +0x7ce0 (twelve-byte stride); bit 11 sends `0x10406` with the word at +0x7fe0; bit 12 sends the settle sequence, `0x10018` with no operand, and the settle sequence again; bits 18, 19 and 20 each send sixteen blocks of sixteen words, blocks 0x220 to 0x22f from +0x7fe4, 0x230 to 0x23f from +0x83e4 and 0x240 to 0x24f from +0x87e4; bit 22 sends two runs of sixteen blocks of sixteen words, blocks 0x200 to 0x20f from +0x8d7c and 0x210 to 0x21f from +0x917c; bit 23 sends `0x10c1c` with three words from +0x957c; bit 24 sends `0x10451` with the word at +0x9698; bit 25 sends `0x10415` with the word at +0x7c04; bit 21 sends the settle sequence and then either `0x90008116` or, when bit 27 of the word at +0x96a4 is set, blocks 0x041, 0x04b and 0x040 with zero operands (the second with 0x00ffffff in its second operand), followed by `0x11017` with four words from +0x969c.
  Bits 16 and 17 choose among three layouts by a mode word. Bit 16 reads the mode at +0x8ce8: mode 1 sends blocks 0x030 to 0x033 of eight words each from +0x8cec (0x20-byte stride), mode 2 sends block 0x03b with eight words from +0x8cec, mode 3 sends `0x1083d` with two words. Bit 17 reads the mode at +0x8be4: mode 1 sends blocks 0x028 to 0x02b of sixteen words each, mode 2 block 0x038 with sixteen words, mode 3 `0x1103a` with four words.
  The contents of blocks 0x028 to 0x02b, 0x038 and 0x03a are **unknown**; `docs/buzz.md` (the second three-layout set) lists the routes tried.
- `__glOdysseyInitDefaultValues` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd969ac0, 3548 bytes): **established.**
  Puts a new context into the state of the GL specification's initial values and marks the board copy stale. It zeroes the three words of block 0x3e (+0x8d6c to +0x8d74), sets the identity post-filter (+0x8cec is 0x1000, +0x8cf0 is 0, mode +0x8ce8 is 3), zeroes the sixteen-word area at +0x8be8 and sets its mode word +0x8be4 to 3, writes the LOD block words at +0x9724 to +0x9730 (0, 0, 0x3100, 0xf00), and ORs the matching dirty bits 0x8000, 0x10000, 0x20000, 0x4000, 0x800000 and 0x2000 into +0x16960 (block `0x10c1c` at +0x957c takes 0, 0x1000aaa and 0x2aa). It selects the front or back draw buffer by calling `__glOdyim_DrawBuffer` with 0x404 or 0x405 according to the byte at +0xc96, and sends `0x10046` followed by the image-state initialiser `__glOdysseyImageInit`; only when that returns nonzero does it continue, so a failed image set-up leaves the context unusable.
  The rest is a list of calls to the immediate-mode entry points with the specification's defaults: it disables the capabilities with enumerants 0xb10, 0xb42, 0x2a01, 0x2a02, 0x8037, 0xb44, 0x8400, 0xc11, 0xbc0, 0xb90, 0xb71, 0xbe2, 0xbf1, 0xbf2, 0xb60, 0xde0, 0xde1, 0x806f and 0xb50, enables 0xbd0, and sets cull face 0x405, front face 0x901, shade model 0x1d01, polygon mode 0x408 with 0x1b02, alpha function 0x207, stencil function 0x207 and operation 0x1e00 three times, depth function 0x201, logic op 0x1503, blend equation 0x8006, blend function (1, 0), line stipple (1, 0xffff), line width and point size 1.0 and depth range 0 to 1. It sets colour, edge flag, normal, texture coordinate and the front-and-back material and fragment-material terms, the light-environment and fragment-light parameters of the SGIX fragment-lighting extension, then calls `__glOdysseyInitLighting`, `__glOdysseyInitFragmentLighting`, `__glOdysseyInitFragmentMaterial` and `__glOdysseyTextureDriverInit`.
  It also sets the four words at +0x7c60 to +0x7c6c to 0xffffff, 0xffffff, 0xffffff and 0xff, stores 0xd at +0x982c and sends that word to the FIFO, marks all of +0x16964 dirty (0xffffffff), and sets the byte at +0x21ef8 to 1 when an environment variable (named by a string in the library's data pool) is present, 0 otherwise; the program-select routines below test that byte.
- `__glOdysseyValidateDispatchTable` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd970d30, 1844 bytes): **established.**
  Chooses which of the library's entry-point tables the context's dispatch table at +0x17f4 is filled with, from the clip state, and copies it in. It reads the number of software clip planes through `__glOdysseyGetNumSWClipPlanes` and, when +0xc84 is 0x1c00, calls `__glOdysseyValidateSoftClipPlanes`. The result is a mode number kept at +0x1695c: 1 when +0xc84 is not 0x1c00; otherwise 0 (no software clip planes, word at +0x16980 zero), 3 (no software clip planes, that word nonzero), 2 (software clip planes, that word zero) or 4 (software clip planes, that word nonzero). It returns early when the mode is unchanged and the byte at +0x19dc0 is clear.
  The table is one of three sets (modes 0 and 3, modes 2 and 4, or mode 1, which uses a single table), and within a set one of four tables chosen by the byte at +0xc94 (colour-index or RGBA visual) and the word at +0x16980. The routine copies it with `__glMipsCopyDispatch` and calls two unnamed helpers at 0xd9704c0 and 0xd970bd0 around the copy (not read). For mode 0 it then refreshes the projection class at +0x19dbc when the byte at +0x19dc0 is set (0 when the byte at +0x19db4 is clear, 3 when the word at +0x19db8 is 0x1102, else 2, lowered to 1 when class 2 has zero terms at +0xbc, +0xcc and +0xdc of the matrix record and the magnitude of +0xec exceeds that of the float at +0x19d90) and overwrites the 24 vertex entries (+0x528 to +0x584, Vertex2d through Vertex4sv): class 2 takes `__glOdyim_vertexSmallWDispatchTable`, class 1 replaces the eight Vertex4 entries with the `_smallw` forms, class 3 takes `__glOdyim_vertexMagDispatchTable` when bit 22 of the word at +0x7b70 is set and `__glOdyim_vertexMagLine0DispatchTable` otherwise. For modes 2 and 4 it points the context's primitive-begin entries (+0x2f34 to +0x2f58) at the soft-clip forms (`__glOdysseySoftClipBeginPoints` and its siblings) and the procedure pickers (+0x2e48 to +0x2e94) at the soft-clip pickers, using the fast triangle picker when the bytes at +0x19de2 and +0xc94 allow. For the other modes it writes the generic `__glBegin*` entries and pickers. It ends by setting the global at address 0x1018 to 2 and bit 0 of the word at +0x2e24, which flag that validation has more to do.
- `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixComb` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e2f90, 1652 bytes): **established.**
  Rebuilds the combined viewport and projection numbers the board needs after a transform or viewport change. It forms two four-float vectors at +0x9588 to +0x95a4 from the matrix record at +0x7414 and the viewport words at +0x6b8 to +0x6c4, calls `__glOdysseyUpdateTransform` first when the byte at +0x16740 asks for it, and then computes, from the near and far distances at +0x165c8 and +0x165d0, the cached scale terms at +0x165f8 to +0x16610 and the words at +0x6c8 and +0x6cc, the rows stored at +0x95a8 to +0x95d4 (each a combination of the matrix's third and fourth columns, +0xb8 to +0xec of the record); the matrix's fourth-column terms +0xbc, +0xcc, +0xdc and +0xec are copied unchanged to +0x95b8 to +0x95c4. The 20 words from +0x9588 to +0x95d4 go to the board as follows: `0x34008000` selecting table index 0 of register 0x34, the word `0x9080bdc8` and the sixteen floats from +0x9588, then `0x34008000 | enc(16)`, the word `0x908091df` and the four floats from +0x95c8, then a reselect of index 0 (these are the register-0x34 table slots that `docs/buzz.md` lists for the kernel's restore).
  It then sets the projection class at +0x19dbc (the same classes `__glOdysseyValidateDispatchTable` reads): when the byte at +0x19dc0 is set it takes 0 if the byte at +0x19db4 is clear, 3 if the word at +0x19db8 is 0x1102 and 2 otherwise. When +0x19db4 is set and +0x19db8 is 0x1101 it picks 2 if any of +0xbc, +0xcc and +0xdc is nonzero, and when all three are zero it picks 2 or 1 by comparing the magnitude of +0xec with the float at +0x19d90, setting the word at +0x16738 to 1 in the all-zero cases. A change of class sets the byte at +0x19dc0 and bit 10 of +0x16964. It sets bit 4 of +0x16988 for classes 2 and 3 and clears it otherwise, ORs 1 and a comparison of the doubles at +0x6a8 and +0x6b0 (shifted to bit 1) into +0x9698, marks dirty bit 24 of +0x16960 so that block `0x10451` is resent, and records the in-progress marker 2 in the global at 0x1018.
- `__glOdysseyUpdateTransform` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e2980, 1544 bytes): **established.**
  Analyses the current projection matrix and stores the eye-space distances the depth mapping needs. It reads the projection matrix record at +0x741c and the model-view record at +0x7414. When the byte at +0x19dcc is set, the projection's flag word at +0x40 is 4 and the model-view record has a nonzero term at +0xc, +0x1c or +0x2c, it copies the 4 x 4 at +0xb0 of that record with its fourth column set to 0, 0, 0 and 1.0, takes its inverse transpose with `__glInvertTransposeMatrix`, multiplies the projection's fourth row (+0xc, +0x1c, +0x2c, +0x3c) through it to get the four terms used below, and sets the byte at +0x19dcd to 1. Otherwise it clears that byte and reads the matrix terms directly, replacing a projection with a skew by its scale factors, the square roots of the sums of squares of the column terms at +0x8, +0x18 and +0x28 and at +0xc, +0x1c and +0x2c, signed by the sign of the diagonal entries +0x28 and +0x2c.
  The four numbers it ends with (the two scales and the two translations) are cached in doubles at +0x165f8, +0x16600, +0x16608 and +0x16610 and the routine reuses them without recomputing when the terms at +0x28, +0x2c, +0x38 and +0x3c already equal the cached values and the terms at +0x8, +0xc, +0x18 and +0x1c are zero. From them it derives the near and far distances n and f and writes them at +0x165c8 and +0x165d0, picking between the formulas for an orthographic matrix (the second scale zero) and a perspective matrix (the sum and difference of the scales). It sets the word at +0x1673c to 1 when the matrix at +0x7428 has a nonzero perspective column, clears the byte at +0x16740, calls the unnamed routine at 0xd9e4d90, and calls `__glOdyim_PolygonOffsetEXT` again when either of the offset factors at +0x1f4 and +0x1f8 is nonzero, so that the polygon offset follows the new depth scale.

- `__glOdysseyValidateContext` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9697f0, 716 bytes): **established.**
  Runs the validators for whatever changed in the context and then pushes the result to the board. The change word is +0x16964. A mask of bits to leave for later is built first: bit 0x2000 stays pending unless the byte at +0x16974 is set, and when the byte at +0x19de3 is 1 the bits in 0x59ff (0x58ff when the byte at +0x16976 is 1) stay pending while the bytes at +0x16974 and +0x16975 are clear, and bit 0x4000 is dropped from the pending set when the byte at +0x16977 is 1 and +0x16975 is clear. The rest are handled in this order: 0x4000 calls `__glOdysseyValidateDepthRange`, 0x100 `__glOdysseyValidateProgram`, 0x200 `__glOdysseyValidateTextureState`, 0x800 `__glOdysseyValidateContextRasterMisc`, 1 `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixComb`, 2 `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixMV`, 4 `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixNorm`, 8 `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixTex`, 0x10 `__glOdysseyValidateClipPlanes` (and `__glOdysseyValidateSoftClipPlanes` when the software plane count is above zero), 0x20 `__glOdysseyValidateTexGen`, 0x40 `__glOdysseyValidateLighting` then `__glOdysseyComputeLightState`, 0x80 `__glOdysseyValidateFragment`, 0x1000 `__glOdysseyValidateFog`, 0x400 `__glOdysseyValidateDispatchTable` and 0x2000 `__glOdysseyValidateScissor`.
  Between the 0x400 and 0x2000 steps, if any non-pending bit is still set in +0x16964 (the validators set some), it calls `__glOdysseySetProgram` with the current program number from +0xa6a8. It then stores the pending mask back as the new change word and, when the board-state word at +0x16960 is nonzero, calls `__glOdysseyValidateHardware`; with no live bit it only stores the pending mask.
- `__glOdysseyValidateContextRasterMisc` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd969500, 488 bytes): **established.**
  Recomputes three small state words from the enable words +0x700 and +0x704. Bit 17 of the word at +0x7bfc is set when bit 1 of +0x700 is set and bit 11 of +0x704 is clear, and dirty bit 1 of +0x16960 is raised. Bit 29 of the descriptor word at +0x96a4 is kept at 1 only while none of bits 0x8017 of +0x700 and bit 0x800 of +0x704 are set; a change there raises dirty bit 0x200000 of +0x16960. The two words at +0x7c54 and +0x7c58 get a 24-bit field each, computed as 128.0 times a float (+0x1d8 and +0x1cc) when bit 9 or bit 10 of +0x700 is set and times an integer (+0x1dc and +0x1d0) otherwise, truncated to an integer; dirty bit 0x40 is raised for them.
- `__glOdysseyValidateScissor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9696f0, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Sends the scissor rectangle to the board when it changed. It compares the two words at +0x7c20 and +0x7c24 (the operands of block `0x1081d`) with the words at +0x165b4 and +0x165b8; if they differ it copies the new values in, sends the settle prefix when the flag at +0x9830 is set, and sends `0x1081d` with the two words. When bit 20 of the word at +0x7b70 is clear it also calls `__glOdysseyIoctl` with request 0x6f64000d and four 12-bit values taken from the two words: the high halves of the first and second word, then their low halves.
- `__glOdysseyValidateClipPlanes` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd967e40, 832 bytes): **established.**
  Sends the changed user clip planes to the board. For each of the 13 plane slots whose bit is set in the plane-change word +0x70c it copies the plane's four floats from the plane array that the word at +0x6d4 points to (16 bytes a slot) into a packed shadow at +0x9cb4 and sends register 0x31 selecting table index 0x120 plus 4 for each plane sent so far, the word `0x908091bb` and the four floats. If at least one plane was sent it reselects the saved index kept at +0xa218 with `0x31008000 | enc(index)`. Afterwards it sets bit 2 of +0x16988 when `__glOdysseyGetNumSWClipPlanes` is nonzero (clears it otherwise), writes the number of hardware planes from `__glOdysseyGetNumHWClipPlanes` into bits 2:0 of +0x7c00 as three thermometer bits (bit 0 when more than two, bit 1 when more than one, bit 2 when any), and raises dirty bit 4 of +0x16960.
- `__glOdysseyValidateDepthRangeNewXform` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e4d90, 376 bytes): **established.**
  Refreshes the depth range and the near and far clip planes after the projection analysis changed the eye-space distances. It raises GL error 0x502 and returns when the global at 0x1018 is 1. When the requested window range at +0x165d8 and +0x165e0 is not the unset marker -1.0 at +0x165d8, it converts both ends with `__glOdysseyWindowToNormalizedEyeZ` and calls `__glim_DepthRange` with the results. Then, if bit 7 of the plane-change word at +0x70c is set, it rewrites plane slot 7 of the plane array that the word at +0x6d4 points to as (0, 0, s, s x n) with s = -1.0 when n is below f and 1.0 otherwise (n and f are the doubles at +0x165c8 and +0x165d0), and if bit 6 is set it rewrites slot 6 the same way with the opposite sign rule and f in place of n; each rewrite raises bit 4 of +0x16964 and sets the global at 0x1018 to 2.
- `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixMV` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e3610, 368 bytes): **established.**
  Sends the model-view matrix to the board. It copies the 4 x 4 float matrix at the record +0x7414 into the shadow at +0x95d8 (first column of the source, then second, and so on, so the shadow holds the transpose), then sends register 0x34 selecting table index 36 (`0x34008000 | enc(36)`), the word `0x9080bdc8` and the sixteen floats, and reselects index 0.
- `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixTex` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e3a20, 368 bytes): **established.**
  Sends the texture matrix to the board in the same form as the model-view matrix: it copies the 4 x 4 float matrix at the record +0x7428 transposed into the shadow at +0x9658, then sends register 0x34 selecting table index 20, the word `0x9080bdc8`, the sixteen floats, and a reselect of index 0.
- `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixNorm` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e3780, 660 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the normal matrix from the model-view record and sends it. It first calls the record's pending-update routine through the pointer at +0x2eb4 when the byte at +0x10c of the record at +0x7414 is set. It takes a scale factor: 1.1415927 (the constant at 0xdaf4c84) when bit 22 of +0x700 is set; otherwise 1.0, except when bit 16 of +0x704 is set, when it is the reciprocal of the length of the vector at +0x78, +0x7c and +0x80 of the record (a rescale-normal factor). It copies the twelve floats at +0x58 to +0x90 of the record (a 3 x 4 block, transposed in the same way as the other matrices) into the shadow at +0x9618, multiplying each by the factor unless it is exactly 1.0, then sends register 0x34 selecting table index 52, the word `0x9080bdc8` and the sixteen words, and reselects index 0.
- `__glOdysseyValidateFog` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd980cd0, 1800 bytes): **established.**
  Turns the GL fog state into the three fog words of block 0x1a (+0x7c10, +0x7c14, +0x7c18 and +0x7c1c) and marks dirty bit 8. Fog is described to the board as a cubic curve through four control values over the eye-distance range. It calls `__glOdysseyUpdateTransform` first when the byte at +0x16740 asks, and takes n and f from +0x165c8 and +0x165d0. By the mode word at +0x634: for GL_EXP (0x800) and GL_EXP2 (0x801) it takes the density at +0x648, limits the range end to 3.912 / density for exp (where the factor is 0.02) and to 1.83096 / density for exp2, samples exp at the range's start, one third, two thirds and end, and turns the four samples into cubic control values with the interior ones (18 s1 - 9 s2 + 2 s3 - 5 s0) / 6 and (18 s2 - 9 s1 - 5 s3 + 2 s0) / 6; for GL_LINEAR (0x2601) the control values are the straight line 1, 2/3, 1/3, 0 or its reverse, with the start and end at +0x64c and +0x650 and the slope word at +0x654 choosing the direction; for 0x812a (the fog-function extension) the four values come from +0x16454 to +0x16460, the scale from +0x16468 and the offset from +0x16464. When bit 4 of +0x704 is set it subtracts the offset word at +0x16478.
  It then converts to window depth using the depth range doubles at +0x6a8 and +0x6b0, folds the scale and offset into an exponent (a 3-bit power of two chosen with `logf` and `ceilf` so the offset stays within +-8) and packs: bits 23:0 of +0x7c10 take the float bits of the scale rebiased by 0xd0000000 and shifted right 5, bits 11:0 of +0x7c14 the offset times 256 and bits 14:12 the exponent, +0x7c18 and +0x7c1c take the control values times 256 in 12-bit fields (first and second control value in +0x7c18 bits 23:12 and 11:0, third and fourth in +0x7c1c bits 23:12 and 11:0). An offset below -8 or above 7.996 is brought into range by the exponent, and one beyond -1024 or 1023.5 is replaced by -8 or 7.996 with the scale reduced by the same ratio.

## Primitive replay for the clip path

- `__glOdysseyVertexWARClip` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ef330, 4452 bytes): **established.**
  Called with one vertex (four float arguments) when a primitive started on the board must be finished through the software clip path; it ends the board's primitive, rebuilds the transform state the software path needs, and re-issues the primitive's held vertices through the immediate-mode entry points, ending with the vertex it was called for. It first asks the hardware-shadow routine for the board's primitive state (`__glOdysseyGetHardwareShadow`) and copies 0x900 bytes of it to its stack. The word at +0x80 of that copy holds the primitive mode in bits 3:0 (a value of 10 or more makes it print a message and send nothing), the number of vertices held in bits 5:4, the index of the oldest held vertex in bits 7:6 (the held vertices form a ring of four) and a strip-parity flag in bit 11.
  It sets the byte at +0x169fe, for mode 2 sets the settle flag at +0x9830 and sends `0x10444` with operand 0, calls the dispatch table's end entry (+0x2c, inferred End) and zeroes the pair at +0x80 and +0x84 of the record at +0x6d4. The words at +0x88 and +0x8c of that record take -1.0 and 1.0 when the byte at +0x19dcd is set, and otherwise a unit step whose sign follows the order of the far and near distances at +0x165d0 and +0x165c8, and that step multiplied by n. When the projection class at +0x19dbc is 3 it also writes four clip planes into the record at +0x6d4, from +0x90 to +0xcc: for the x and y rows of the projection matrix at +0x741c the planes are 0.9 times the row's x (or y) term plus its w term, and w minus 0.9 times the term, so they sit at 1/0.9 of the viewing volume's x and y bounds (the constant is 0.9 in the library's double pool at 0xdaf4fc0). It sets bits 8 to 12 of the clip dirty word at +0x70c and bits 4, 8 and 10 of +0x16964, sets the global at 0x1018 to 2 and the in-replay bytes +0x19d8e and +0x19dc8, and clears the two bytes at the end.
  The replay is a switch on the mode. For each mode it calls the dispatch table's begin entry (+0x1c, inferred Begin) with the mode (a line loop temporarily swaps the entry at +0x19ea0 for the partial soft-clip loop-begin routine around that call), sends each held vertex with `__glOdysseySendShadowAttributes` and `__glOdysseySendShadowVertex`, then the current attributes (index -1) and the argument vertex through the Vertex4f entry at +0x570 (inferred). With fewer than two vertices held every case replays the held ones; with more, the polygon and fan cases replay the oldest held vertex and one chosen by a modulo-3 step of the ring index, the quad cases replay the vertices at ring offsets 1, 2 and 3 from the oldest or the last two, and the strip case with the parity flag set replays two held vertices and the new one as a one-triangle primitive begun in mode 4, ends it, and restarts mode 5 from the last held vertex and the new one.
- `__glOdysseySendShadowAttributes` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9eea40, 1904 bytes): **established.**
  Re-sends the per-vertex GL attributes recorded in a hardware-shadow copy for one vertex slot (argument three: a slot number, or -1 for the current attributes). It selects the slot's word of packed 2-bit indices at +0x8c + 8 x slot (or +0x84) and uses each pair of bits to choose one of four saved values of an attribute, then calls the dispatch table to set it. Order: front-face ambient, diffuse, specular, emission (copies at +0x500, +0x540, +0x580, +0x5c0, 16 bytes apart) and shininess (+0x600, squared before sending, so the shadow keeps the square root the board takes) through `Materialfv` (slot +0x80, arguments 0x404, the pname 0x1200, 0x1201, 0x1202, 0x1600 or 0x1601, and a pointer); the same five for the back face (0x405, from +0x680 to +0x780); then the colour, either a colour-index entry (+0x610 or +0x618, chosen by the type code 6 or 7 in the word at +0x110 to +0x11c) when the byte at +0xc94 is clear, or one of fourteen colour entries (Color3 and Color4 in the byte, short, int and float forms, slots +0x588 to +0x5f8, chosen by the opcode in the word at +0x120 minus 8); then the texture coordinate (opcodes 0x1e to 0x25 choosing the TexCoord1 to TexCoord4 entries, slots +0x660 to +0x6c8, from the record at +0x440); then the normal (opcodes 0x16 to 0x19: Normal3b, Normal3s, Normal3i and Normal3f at +0x630, +0x650, +0x648 and +0x640, from +0x3c0); and last the edge flag (+0x24, called with 1 for opcode 0xf and 0 for 0x10). An opcode outside its group prints a message and sends nothing for that group.
  Attribute numbers, read from the selector bits and ring addresses in the listing (`libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/ody_clip.s`, source lines of the routine at 0xd9eea40; **established** for the arithmetic, **inferred** for the attribute roles). Attribute A's 2-bit selector is at bit 2 x (16 - A) of the first descriptor word for A up to 16 and at bit 2 x (32 - A) of the second word for 17 and 18; its command words sit at 0x100 + 16 A and its operands at 0x300 + 64 A. The routine reads: A = 1 colour index (words at +0x110, type code 6 or 7), 2 colour (words +0x120, selector bits 29:28, operand opcode minus 8 picks the entry), 3 normal (words +0x130, selector bits 27:26, opcodes 0x16 to 0x19, operands +0x3c0), 5 texture coordinate (words +0x150, selector bits 23:22, opcodes 0x1e to 0x25, operands +0x440), 7 edge flag (words +0x170, bits 19:18, bits 28:24 of the first word 15 or 16), 8 to 11 front ambient, diffuse, specular, emission (selector bits 17:16, 15:14, 13:12, 11:10; operands +0x500, +0x540, +0x580, +0x5c0), 12 front shininess (bits 9:8, operands +0x600), 14 to 16 back ambient, diffuse, specular (bits 5:4, 3:2, 1:0), 17 back emission (second word bits 31:30) and 18 back shininess (bits 29:28). It never reads A = 4, 6, 13 or 19. So the current GL texture coordinate is attribute 5, the one the kernel's console message calls texcoordA; attribute 4 is not the GL texture coordinate.
- `__glOdysseySendShadowVertex` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ef1b0, 380 bytes): **established.**
  Re-sends the position of one held vertex from a hardware-shadow copy (arguments: the context, the shadow copy and a slot number). Slot s has a type code in the word at +0x100 + 4 s (low 10 bits) and up to four words at +0x300 + 16 s. Types 0 to 5 call the Vertex2i, Vertex2f, Vertex3i, Vertex3f, Vertex4i and Vertex4f entries at +0x538, +0x530, +0x558, +0x550, +0x578 and +0x570 with two, three or four of those words, which agrees with the integer-then-float order of the vertex opcodes 0 to 5 in `BUZZ.h`. Any other type prints a message with the type.

## Programs

- `__glOdysseySetProgram` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c9ee0, 1220 bytes): **established.**
  Makes the transform program numbered by its argument the one the board runs, loading microcode records when the program needs a different overlay, and sends the registers that start it. When the byte at +0x21ef8 is set it sends only `0x298080c5` (routine 1221 of the transform image, `docs/tokens.md`); when the program count at +0xa654 is zero it sends nothing. A word of the form `0x27008000 | enc(a)` sets the instruction write address to a and `0x40008000 | enc(h)` writes one 16-bit piece of an instruction, with enc(v) = (v & 0x3ff) | (v >> 10) << 23 (`ucode/buzz_isa.md`); each record is six halfwords, sent from the sixth to the first, from the table at +0x16d34 with a 12-byte stride.
  In order: it rewrites the previously patched instruction (address at +0xa6b0, original record number derived from it and the loaded overlay start at +0xa6ac) back to its original contents; stores the program number at +0xa6a8; takes the program's 32-byte record at +0xa658 + 32 x number; when the record's overlay start (record word +0x14) is nonzero and differs from +0xa6ac, loads min(count at +0x16d38 minus that start, 199) records from that image record to write addresses 1848, 1849 and so on, and stores the new start at +0xa6ac; stores the record's patch address (word +0x18) at +0xa6b0 and writes the image record named by word +0x1c to that address; then sends register 0x29 with the list entry at +0xa254 + 4 x (record word +0x10), register 0x33 with that index plus one, the block `0x90808800` with two zero operands, and registers 0x33, 0x29, 0x2a and 0x2c with the record's words +0xc, +0x0, +0x4 and +0x8, each as `0x2n008000 | enc(value)` (n is the register, `0x33008000` for 0x33). It keeps copies of those values in the shadow words +0xa1f8, +0xa1fc, +0xa204 and +0xa220.
- `__glOdysseyValidateProgram` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c9d90, 332 bytes): **established.**
  Rebuilds the list of program entry points and sends it to the board. When the byte at +0x21ef8 is set it sends only `0x298080c5`. Otherwise it saves the current program number from +0xa6a8, clears that word and the list length at +0xa654, calls `AddProgram` (0xd9c8880) with 0, and again with 1 when `__glOdysseyGetNumSWClipPlanes` is nonzero, so the list at +0xa254 holds the entry points for the current state, restores the program number, and then sends for each list position i the pair `0x28008000 | enc(i)` and `0x60008000 | enc(entry)` (enc(v) = (v & 0x3ff) | (v >> 10) << 23). It finishes by storing the program number in the four words +0xa698 to +0xa6a4 that record the previous programs. `ucode/buzz_isa.md` describes the list as it reaches the board.
## Texture

- `__glOdysseyTextureActivate` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d1f90, 1448 bytes): **established.**
  Makes a texture object the one the board samples (arguments: the context and the texture object); returns 1 on success and 0 on failure. It returns 0 at once when the object's board-memory handle at +0x4fc is -1. It takes the last level to bind as the smaller of the object's maximum level (+0x60) and +0x9c (for a 3D object, target 0x806f, the base level at +0x5c), opens a reference to the object in the board-memory manager (`__glOdysseyOpenPMObjReference`, handle, base and last level, and the object's per-level offsets at +0x514) and raises GL error 0x505 (out of memory) when that fails. On success it records the object at +0x16418 and the level range at +0x16420 and +0x16424.
  For each level from the base to the last it writes the three-word level descriptor at +0x7ce0 + 12 x (level - base): word 0 takes the level's board-memory offset from +0x514 + 4 x level into bits 28:0, word 1 takes the format index (+0x554, bits 25:20), the +0x558 field (bits 31:30) and the row length in 16-texel units rounded up to an even number (bits 11:0, with 4096 stored as bit 0), and word 2 takes, for a 3D object, the ceiling of the base-2 logarithm of the row length times the number of rows, both in 16-texel units (bits 11:0), and 0 for other targets. It then derives a 3-bit class from +0x554 and the first level's format at +0x56c with the unnamed routine at 0xd9cfc80 and stores it in bits 25:23 of the object's mode word at +0xb4. For the detail-texture filter modes (the filter field of +0xb4 equal to 2, 3 or 4) it opens a second reference to the detail object at +0x16410 and writes its descriptors from +0x7fe0; a failed open raises error 0x505, deactivates the object and returns 0. It ends by clearing bits 5:0 of the word at +0x7fe0, ORing 0x1c00 into the dirty word at +0x16960 and setting the global at 0x1018 to 2.
- `__glOdysseyTextureDeactivate` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d2540, 276 bytes): **established.**
  Releases the object that `__glOdysseyTextureActivate` bound (arguments: the context and the object). It closes the board-memory reference through `__glOdysseyClosePMObjReference` with the level range kept at +0x16420 and +0x16424, clears the active-object pointer at +0x16418, and overwrites the object's sixteen per-level offsets at +0x514 to +0x550 with the poison value 0xdeadbeef so a stale use is visible. When a detail object is bound at +0x1641c it does the same for it with the range at +0x16428 and +0x1642c and the poison value 0xbaadcade, and clears that pointer.
- `__glOdysseyValidateTexture` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d26d0, 1236 bytes): **established.**
  Checks one texture object for use by the board and fills the words of its texture-mode block (arguments: the context and the object); its result, stored at +0x10 of the object, is 0 (unusable), 1 (usable) or 2 (needs another validation). After the unnamed routine at 0xd9d2660 it handles the detail-texture magnification filters (the word at +0x18 equal to 0x8097, 0x8098 or 0x8099): for a 2D object whose format at +0x554 matches the detail object's and whose first level is not empty it copies the detail object's two parameters from +0x90 and +0x94 (five bits each) into bits 24:20 and 19:15 of +0xa4, and otherwise it sets the filter field of +0xb4 to 1; for other targets it clears bit 20 of +0xa0 and does the same. It then sets bit 9 of +0xa0.
  It computes the three level counts n = max(0, size level - base level) from +0x90, +0x94 and +0x98 minus the base level at +0x5c, packs them into +0xa4 (bits 14:10, 9:5 and 4:0 for the first, second and third axis), and writes into +0xa8, +0xac and +0xb0 the product of 2^n and the object's clamp floats at +0x48, +0x4c and +0x50, truncated to 17 bits (`docs/buzz.md` has the field reading of these words). A level-0 width, height or depth of zero returns 0. It requires the wrap word at +0x28 to be 0x2901 (GL repeat), calling the unnamed routine at 0xd9d5d20 when it is not (for a 3D object it compares +0x28 with +0x24 instead), and then the result depends on the minification filter at +0x14: for 0x2600, 0x2601 and 0x8146 (nearest, linear and the four-tap filter) a target other than 1D or 2D is valid at once, and a 1D or 2D one asks the unnamed routine at 0xd9d1dc0 and returns 1 on success or, on failure, sets bit 14 of +0xa0, clears +0xcc and returns 2. For the mipmap filters it walks the levels up to the smaller of +0x60 and +0x9c, halving each size (minimum 1, border removed) and comparing every stored level's format, border (+0x5c0 + 0x44 x level) and sizes with what a complete mipmap needs, returning 0 at the first mismatch and 1 when all match.
- `__glOdysseyValidateTextureState` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d2bb0, 1204 bytes): **established.**
  Chooses which of the 1D, 2D and 3D texture objects the board samples and copies that object's board words into the context. The enable word at +0x700 decides: bit 30 selects the 3D object (pointer at +0x1640c), else bit 17 the 2D object (+0x16408), else bit 16 the 1D object (+0x16404). A candidate with the state value 2 at +0x10 is first validated with `__glOdysseyValidateTexture`; one that still has 0 is skipped. The chosen dimension sets bits 5, 4 or 3 (3D, 2D, 1D) and bit 10 in the word at +0x7c00 and bit 2 of the dirty word, and when no object qualifies the routine clears bits 10, 11, 5, 4 and 3 there instead, deactivates any bound object and returns.
  If the chosen object is not the one bound at +0x16418 it ORs 0x11c200 into +0x16960, deactivates the bound object, and activates the new one with `__glOdysseyTextureActivate`; on success it copies the object's words into the context: +0xb4 (masked into +0x7cd4), +0xa4 to +0xb0 into +0x7cc4 to +0x7cd0, +0xb8 and +0xbc into +0x7cd8 and +0x7cdc (the seven words of block 0x16), +0xc0 to +0xcc into +0x9724 to +0x9730 (block 0x13), +0xd0 to +0xd8 into +0x8d6c to +0x8d74 (block 0x3e), the eight post-filter words +0xdc to +0xf8 into +0x8cec to +0x8d08 with the post-filter mode at +0x8ce8 set to 2, and 256 words from +0xfc to +0x87e4 to +0x8be0 (the table sent by dirty bit 20). It ORs the object's own dirty word (+0xa0) into +0x16960, clears it, and sets the global at 0x1018 to 2.
- `__glOdysseyTextureDriverInit` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9cf7a0, 460 bytes): **established.**
  Initialises the context's seven texture objects and the active-texture pointers. It runs the object initialiser at 0xd9cf5a0 with level 0 on the records at +0x120f8 (target 0xde0, 1D), +0x12a8c (0xde1, 2D), +0x13420 (0x806f, 3D), +0x13db4 (0x8095), +0x14748 (0x8063), +0x150dc (0x8064) and +0x15a70 (0x8070), each 0x994 bytes after the last. - `__glOdysseyTextureSetMinFilter` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d5e40, 860 bytes): **established.**
  Applies a minification filter to a texture object (arguments: the object and the filter enumerant). It stores the enumerant at +0x14 of the object and a filter code in bits 7:5 of the mode word at +0xb4: 0x2600 gives 0, 0x2601 gives 1, 0x2700 gives 2, 0x2701 gives 3, 0x2702 gives 4 and 0x2703 gives 5; for the first two the code is 2 and 5 when the object's magnification filter at +0x18 is one of the detail filters 0x8097, 0x8098 or 0x8099. The value 0x832a is accepted too, with code 1, and bit 17 of +0xb4 is set whenever either filter is 0x832a and cleared otherwise. The mipmap filters call the unnamed routine at 0xd9d5de0. Any other enumerant raises GL error 0x500. On success it sets the object's state word at +0x10 to 2 (needs validation) and raises bit 9 of its dirty word at +0xa0.
- `__glOdysseyTextureSetMagFilter` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d61a0, 356 bytes): **established.**
  Applies a magnification filter to a texture object (arguments: the object and the enumerant). It stores the enumerant at +0x18 and a code in bits 4:1 of the mode word +0xb4: 0x2600 gives 0, 0x2601 gives 1, 0x8097 gives 2, 0x8098 gives 3, 0x8099 gives 4, and 0x832a gives 1; bit 17 of +0xb4 is set when either filter is 0x832a and cleared otherwise. For the three detail filters it reruns `__glOdysseyTextureSetMinFilter` with the stored minification filter so that the minification code picks the detail variant. Any other enumerant raises GL error 0x500. On success it sets +0x10 to 2 and raises bits 9 and 12 of the dirty word at +0xa0.
- `__glOdysseyTextureSetOp` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d7ad0, 616 bytes): **established.**
  Sets the texture environment function of one texture unit (arguments: the context, the unit number and the function enumerant). It writes a code into bits 2:0 of the first word of block 0x1b at +0x7cb0: GL_REPLACE (0x1e01, or 0x8062) gives 1, GL_MODULATE (0x2100) gives 2, GL_DECAL (0x2101) gives 3, GL_BLEND (0xbe2) gives 4 and GL_ADD (0x104) gives 5; anything else raises GL error 0x500. It saves the enumerant in the unit's 0x24-byte record. For every function except 0x104 it packs the environment colour's four floats (the words at +4, +8, +0xc and +0x10 of the record the pointer at +0x9b4 addresses) through an indirect conversion routine into 12-bit fields of +0x7cb4 (bits 23:12 and 11:0) and +0x7cb8; for 0x104 it sets the low 24 bits of both words to all ones. - `__glOdysseyTextureSetDetailTable` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d8590, 424 bytes): **established.**
  Resamples a list of (position, value) points into the 256-entry table of a texture object (arguments include the object and the point list and its length). For entry k from 0 to 255 it takes the position -k / 32 (the constant 0.03125), finds the segment of the point list that contains it, interpolates linearly between the two points (or uses the first value before the first point and the last value after the last), and stores the result through the helper at 0xd9d84c0 into the table that starts at +0xfc of the object, the table that `__glOdysseyValidateTextureState` copies to +0x87e4.

## Window, board query and visibility tests

- `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9658c0, 496 bytes): **established.**
  Refreshes the context's idea of its window origin and size. When the byte at +0x19de1 is clear it calls the window-geometry callback at +0x1c of the context for the origin and size; when it is set it first asks the kernel with ioctl 0x6f64000f for four integers (origin x and y, width and height) and uses the callback only if that request fails. It stores the origin plus the float at +0x19da4 into the floats at +0x19d9c and +0x19da0, calls `__glChangeWindowSize` when the width or height at +0xe74 and +0xe78 changed, takes the read-window size from the callback at +0x48 (or reuses the draw size when the byte at +0xdd0 is set) and calls `__glChangeReadWindowSize` when +0xe7c or +0xe80 changed, calls `__glChangeWindowSize` again with +0x6a0 and +0x6a4 when the word at +0x16984 is nonzero, and sets the byte at +0x20f4 to 1 when the byte at +0xc98 is set and the size changed.
- `__glOdysseyGetBoardInfoRaw` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d9b90, 444 bytes): **established.**
  Fetches the kernel's board description into the context. It calls the context callback at +0x20 for a handle, sends ioctl 0x66 with a 0x24c-byte reply area, reports an error through the callback at +0x14 when the call returns a negative value, and copies the reply (eight bytes at a time, 0x48 doublewords of 8 bytes plus a trailing word) into the context at +0x16a20.
- `__glOdysseyDriverMakeCurrent` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d9d50, 604 bytes): **established.**
  Asks the kernel to bind the context's drawable and records the answer (returns 1 on success, 0 after reporting an error through the callback at +0x14). It builds a request of two word groups: a flag word of 2 when the word at +0xce4 is positive plus 4 when the byte at +0xc94 is zero, and the same for the second group from +0xd84 and +0xd34. It sends the request with ioctl 0x6f640008 and, on success, copies reply fields into the context at +0x16d40 to +0x16d64, and fills the five-word records at +0x16d68, +0x16d7c, +0x16d90 and +0x16da4 according to a byte of the reply: 0 zeroes the area from +0x16d68 to +0x16db4, 1 fills the first two records and zeroes the rest, and 2 fills all four.
- `__glOdysseyWindowToEyeZ` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9e4710, 376 bytes): **established.**
  Converts a window depth z in 0 to 1 back to an eye-space z (argument: z; result: a float). It calls `__glOdysseyUpdateTransform` first when the byte at +0x16740 asks, maps z to 2 z - 1, and inverts the depth mapping with the cached terms at +0x165f8, +0x16600, +0x16608 and +0x16610 together with n and f from +0x165c8 and +0x165d0. When both scale terms are zero it returns -n; when the second scale term (+0x16600) is zero it divides ((2 z - 1) x the term at +0x16610 minus the term at +0x16608) by the first (+0x165f8); otherwise it uses a quotient of the two forms and keeps the result between -n and -f.
- `__glOdysseyIsBboxClippedRange2` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd974500, 280 bytes): **established.**
  Tests whether a box lies outside the clip volume in the case where the context's projection has no perspective term (the flag word at +0x16738 is set). From the three floats at +0x18, +0x1c and +0x20 of the box record (its argument) and two rows of the matrix shadow it builds two sums, the first from +0x9588, +0x958c and +0x9590 and the second from +0x9598, +0x959c and +0x95a0: each sum starts as the magnitude of the first product, and each later product t is added when it is positive and otherwise replaces the sum with |t| minus the sum. It returns true when either sum exceeds the limit 2 x the float at +0x19d94 x the magnitude of the float at +0x95c4.

## Context creation and lighting

Same build, same method as above. The lighting routines are static functions with no exported name; the names are the compilation unit's own (DWARF). Each reads the assembly of `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY/` with the source-line markers, because the decompiler turned the floating-point multiply-add forms into placeholders and named the static callees after the nearest exported symbol (all of the lighting callees show as `__glOdyim_LightModeli`). Register 0x29, 0x31, 0x32, 0x20 and 0x21 selects below are the words `0xRR008000 | enc(index)`, where enc(i) is `(i & 0x3ff) | (i >> 10) << 23`, as in `docs/tokens.md`.

- `__glOdysseyCreateContext` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd96c610, 12648 bytes): **established.**
  Builds a new GL context record from the visual and share-list arguments. It calls the host's allocator (the callback at +4 of the host import record, arguments 0, 1, 0x21f00) for the 0x21f00-byte record and returns NULL when that fails. It reads the environment variable named by the library's string at 0xdae8b10 (`GL_ODY_FAST_SW_CLIPPING_PLANE`): a value starting with `N` or `n` clears the byte at +0x19de2 and anything else, or no variable, sets it. It then copies the 80-byte host import record to +0, the 160-byte visual description to +0xd30 and from there to +0xc90, and allocates 16 four-byte elements with the same callback, storing the pointer at +0x168d0 and +0x168d4 and zeroing +0x168c8 and +0x168cc; on failure it calls the free callback at +0xc and returns NULL.
  The first context of a process also builds, once (a flag at gp-13616), ten tables of 472 words each in the library's data, one per variant of the immediate-mode entry points: blocks 0 and 1 are the hardware path for an RGBA and a colour-index visual, 2 and 3 the soft-clip pair (block 2 takes the fast soft-clip vertex table), 4 and 5 the hardware pair again with the IrisGL vertex table (`__glOdyim_iglooVertexDispatchTable`), 6 and 7 the soft-clip pair again, 8 the all-software table (`__glSoftim_*`) and 9 the display-list-compile table (`__glOdylc_*`). Each block is a copy of a 330-entry dispatch table followed by six overlay tables copied behind it: vertex (24 entries at +1320), colour (42 at +1416; the index table for a colour-index block), normal (10 at +1584), texture coordinate (32 at +1624), raster position (24 at +1752) and rectangle (8 at +1848). Blocks start 1888 bytes apart. `__glOdysseyValidateDispatchTable` copies one of these blocks into a context's table (the block assignment is **inferred** from the source tables each copy names and the block number that function selects with). The copies were checked by source table, destination offset and entry count rather than instruction by instruction.
  The routine then stores into the new record: +0xe04 = 8, +0xe10 = 13, +0xe18 = 1, +0xe20 and +0xe24 = 3840, five words +0xe28 to +0xe38 = 11, four words +0xe3c to +0xe48 = 256, +0xe4c and +0xe50 = 6144, +0xe8c = 512, +0xe94 = 15, +0xe98 = 3, +0xe9c = 64, the floats 0.5, 10.0 and 0.125 twice at +0xea0 to +0xeb4, +0xeb8 = 30, +0xebc = 4096, +0xec0 and +0xec4 = 16, +0xec8 = 128, +0xecc = 32, +0xed0 and +0xed4 = 10 and +0xed8 = 2. It also sets the display-list compiler, optimiser, initialiser, free and buffer callbacks (+0x1350, +0x134c, +0x1358, +0x1368, +0x13a4, +0x13a8, +0x13ac and the error entry +0x13b0), zeroes eight list-state words between +0x1344 and +0x1370, fills the export table (destroy at +88, lose-current at +92, make-current at +96, make-current-read at +100, the visual matcher at +104, copy-context at +120 and the four hyperpipe entries at +152 to +164) and calls `__glEarlyInitContext`.
  It ends with +0x16a1c = 1 and +0x16a00 to +0x16a08 = 0, and when a share context was passed it calls `__glShareDlist` and `__glOdysseySharePM` with it. The stored limits equal the public sample implementation's defaults for list nesting (64, at +0xe9c), evaluator order (30), name stack (128), modelview depth (32), projection depth (2), texture and colour stacks (10), attribute stacks (16) and the point and line floats (`gfx/lib/opengl/include/machdep.h`); that pairing is how those slots are named, so the names are **inferred** while every stored value is established. The pixel-map limit slot holds 4096 here against the sample's 65536.
- `ValidateLight` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c2ae0, 776 bytes): **established.**
  Turns one GL light's eye-space state into the board's sixteen-float light record and sends it. The arguments are the context, a light state record (116 bytes, the eye position at +0x40 to +0x4c, direction at +0x50, spot exponent +0x60, cutoff +0x64, attenuation +0x68 to +0x70) and a slot number; a float argument in register f16 is added into word 6. Words 0 to 2 hold the position divided by its w when w is nonzero (positional light, words 3 to 6 zero) and otherwise the position scaled to unit length. For a directional light (w = 0) words 3 to 5 hold the negated, normalised half-way vector (the unit light vector plus (0, 0, 1), renormalised) and word 6 is f16 plus the dot product of the unit light vector with that normalised half-way vector. Words 7 to 9 are the constant, linear and quadratic attenuation; words 10 to 12 are the negated, normalised spot direction; word 13 is 1.0 and word 14 is 0 when the light is directional or its cutoff is exactly 180, otherwise word 13 is minus the cosine of the cutoff in radians and word 14 is minus the spot exponent; word 15 is the constant 0x4a800000 (4194304.0). The sixteen words are kept at +0x9834 + 64 x slot, then sent as a select of table index 16 x slot in register 0x31 (stored at +0xa218) followed by the header `0x9080bda2` and the sixteen words. How the board uses the f16 bias and the constant in word 15 is unknown: the only callers pass 0.0 (ordinary lights) and 3.0 (the fragment-lighting slot); the route is the validator's replay of the table, which has no reader in the shipped software.
